    "H2S sorption coefficient in anaerobic layer m^3/kg", 0.1 Sulfide sorption coefficient in anaerobic layer (m³ kg⁻¹)
    Algorithm for resuspension of particulate organic matter for each region (0: Wind Induced Resuspension, 1: Bottom Scour Resuspension),", 1 Choice of resuspension algorithm: 0 = wind induced, 1 = bottom scour
    "Fe(II) Sorption Coefficient in Aerobic Layer m^3/kg", 0.00005 Fe(II) sorption coefficient in aerobic layer (m³ kg⁻¹)
    "Fe(II) Sorption Coefficient in Anaerobic Layer m^3/kg", 0.1 Fe(II) sorption coefficient in anaerobic layer (m³ kg⁻¹). DiToro (2001) used values of 100 to 3000 L kg⁻¹, corresponding to 0.1 to 3 m³ kg⁻¹.
    "Mn(II) Sorption Coefficient in Aerobic Layer m^3/kg", 0.00005 Manganese sorption coefficient in aerobic layer (m³ kg⁻¹)
    "Mn(II) Sorption Coefficient in Anaerobic Layer m^3/kg", 0.1 Manganese sorption coefficient in anaerobic layer (m³ kg⁻¹). DiToro (2001) used a value of 8000 L kg⁻¹ (8 m³ kg⁻¹).

    Fe and Mn Oxidation Rates

    The Fe and Mn oxidation and reduction rates used in the sediment layers are the same values specified in the main control file (w2_con.npt or w2_con.csv) for Fe and Mn. The kinetic rates are adjusted by temperature using a theta value of 1.05.

    Sediment Flux Output Settings¶

    Sample Line Description
    "Write sediment fluxes,", .TRUE. Switch to write sediment fluxes. .TRUE. or .FALSE.

    I/O Control¶

    Sample Line Description
    "Frequency of Sediment Diagenesis Output in days,", 1.0 Frequency that sediment diagenesis output is written to output files (days)

    Temperature Calculations¶

    Sample Line Description
    "Sediment Temperature Computations=1 (internal calcs or =2 neighboring water)" Controls sediment temperature calculation. If set to 1, the sediment temperature uses the temperature defined in the control file and the adjacent water temperature to influence the temperature in the aerobic and anaerobic layers. If set to 2, the adjacent water temperature is used for the sediment temperature.

    Since sediment processes are temperature dependent, the sediment temperature prediction should be reviewed. The initial temperature for both the aerobic and anaerobic layers is set in this input file. The aerobic layer temperature will closely mimic the water temperature in that cell, and the anaerobic layer temperature will approach the sediment temperature (TSED) using the sediment heat exchange coefficient (CBHE).

    An example of a model simulation using an initial temperature of 4 °C, CBHE=0.3 W m⁻² °C⁻¹ (default), and Tsed=13.5 °C is shown in Figure 41. It takes about a year to approach the sediment temperature, hence the 4 °C initial temperature was probably too low for the anaerobic layer. The connectivity between the heat transfer between the two layers is dependent on the pore water diffusion coefficient set in the diagenesis input file. When it is close to molecular, there is little connectivity for temperature between the two layers.

    Output Subdirectory¶

    Sample Line Description
    "Subdirectory for Sediment Diagenesis Output (no quotes needed if no spaces in name)" If blank, the program writes all sediment diagenesis output files to the root directory. Otherwise, specify a subdirectory name (e.g., .\SedDiagOutput) to reduce file clutter.

    Ionization Settings¶

    These are constants hardcoded in the sediment model.

    Constant Value
    Equilibrium constant for NH₄⁺ to NH₃ ionization (pK) 9.1
    Equilibrium constant for HS⁻ to H₂S ionization (pK) 9.0

    Dissolution Settings¶

    These are Henry's law constants hardcoded in the sediment model.

    Constant Value
    Henry's constant for NH₃d to NH₃g (atm M⁻¹) 0.0179
    Henry's constant for CH₄d to CH₄g (atm M⁻¹) 469
    Henry's constant for H₂Sd to H₂Sg (atm M⁻¹) 10
    Henry's constant for CO₂d to CO₂g (atm M⁻¹) 29

    pH Buffering Input File¶

    The pH buffering input file (pH_buffering.npt) provides parameters for dynamic computation of alkalinity. The descriptions of file inputs for the pH and alkalinity routines have been adapted from Sullivan et al. (2013). The variable PHBUFC turns ON/OFF the use of enhanced pH buffering, and NALKC turns ON/OFF the use of non-conservative alkalinity.

    Variables¶

    Variable Description
    PHBUFC ON/OFF: specifies if enhanced pH buffering is used
    NALKC ON/OFF: specifies if non-conservative alkalinity is used
    NH4BUFC ON/OFF: specifies whether ammonia/ammonium is included in pH buffering
    PO4BUFC ON/OFF: specifies whether phosphoric acid is included in pH buffering
    OMBUFC ON/OFF: specifies whether organic matter is included in pH buffering. ON includes both DOM and POM; OFF includes only DOM.
    OMTYPE DIST or MONO: DIST specifies one or more Gaussian distributions of pKa values; MONO specifies a collection of discrete pKa values
    NAG Number of acid/base groups to model, either as means of Gaussian distributions of pKa values or as discrete monoprotic acids
    POMBUFC ON/OFF: specifies whether POM is included in OM buffering
    SDEN Site density, in moles of acid/base sites per mole of carbon in OM
    PK pKa values (negative log10 of the acid dissociation constant), specified as the mean of a distribution or a discrete value
    PKSD Standard deviation for a Gaussian distribution of pKa values (ignored when OMTYPE = MONO)

    Example¶

    Enhanced pH Buffering Input File for CE-QUAL-W2

DYNPHALK  PHBUFC  NCALKC
              ON

BUFTYPE  NH4BUFC PO4BUFC  OMBUFC

OM TYPE   OMTYPE      NAG POMBUFC
            DIST        2     OFF

DENSITY     SDEN    SDEN    SDEN    SDEN    SDEN    SDEN    SDEN    SDEN
SDEN
            0.14    0.10

pK VALS       PK      PK      PK      PK      PK      PK      PK      PK
PK
             4.5     9.6

STD DEV     PKSD    PDSD    PDSD    PDSD    PDSD    PDSD    PDSD    PDSD
PDSD
             1.2     1.0

    Bed Consolidation Rate Input File¶

    If desired, the user can specify the bed consolidation rate as a time-varying input. Input file names are specified by the user in w2_diagenesis.npt. The bed consolidation rate input file is a time series input file with a column header line that is skipped. Additional comment lines can be added at the beginning of the file if they begin with a $. This is a fixed-format file.

    Field Variable Description Units
    1 JDAY Julian day days
    2 Consolidation Bed consolidation rate m day⁻¹

    Example¶

    $Bed consolidation (m/d) rate
JDay    Consolidation
   1.000 0.01370
   2.000 0.01370
   3.000 0.01370
   4.000 0.01370
   5.000 0.01370
   6.000 0.01370

    Atmospheric Deposition Input File¶

    The atmospheric deposition input file (atm_deposition_wbX.csv) specifies wet and dry atmospheric deposition rates for each constituent for each waterbody. Atmospheric deposition is a process where either dry or wet deposition (rain or snow) of pollutants contribute to the mass loading of a pollutant. Dry deposition can include dust or particle transport associated with the pollutant. The processes of atmospheric deposition include Hg, P, N, and other state variables in CE-QUAL-W2.

    For the CE-QUAL-W2 model, the mass deposition rate is in units of kg km⁻² year⁻¹. This unit is often reported by researchers and is more practical than g m⁻² s⁻¹. Internally in the model, unit conversions are applied such that the internal source/sink term units are g s⁻¹ after multiplying by the area. For a variable with units of µg m⁻³ (ng L⁻¹), the atmospheric deposition rates are in units of mg km⁻² year⁻¹.

    The mass load is applied to each waterbody, rather than to each branch, since one would expect the areal deposition to be inclusive of branches and segments making up a waterbody.

    The input format is similar to all other time series input files, in CSV format. The $ character should be the first character in cell A1.

    Field Variable Description Units
    1 JDAY Julian day days
    2+ Constituent names Atmospheric mass deposition rate for each active constituent kg km⁻² year⁻¹

    Typical Atmospheric Deposition Rates¶

    The following table summarizes typical atmospheric deposition rates reported in the literature for mercury (Hg), phosphorus (P), and nitrogen (N). These values provide guidance for setting deposition rates in the absence of site-specific measurements.

    Constituent Deposition Rate Units Source / Notes
    Total Hg (wet) 5--15 mg km⁻² year⁻¹ NADP Mercury Deposition Network, USA
    Total Hg (dry) 5--20 mg km⁻² year⁻¹ Varies by region and proximity to sources
    Total P (wet) 5--30 kg km⁻² year⁻¹ Cole and Wells (2006); Anderson and Downing (2006)
    Total P (dry) 1--15 kg km⁻² year⁻¹ Site-specific; agricultural areas may be higher
    NH₄-N (wet) 100--400 kg km⁻² year⁻¹ NADP National Trends Network
    NO₃-N (wet) 100--350 kg km⁻² year⁻¹ NADP National Trends Network
    Total N (wet + dry) 300--1500 kg km⁻² year⁻¹ Varies by proximity to urban/agricultural sources

    Units for Trace Constituents

    For constituents with units of µg m⁻³ (ng L⁻¹), such as mercury, the atmospheric deposition rates should be specified in units of mg km⁻² year⁻¹ rather than kg km⁻² year⁻¹.

    Example (CSV Format)¶

    $atm_deposition_wb1.csv
Atmospheric mass input, kg/km2/year
JDAY,PO4,NH4,NO3,LPOMP,RPOMP
1,14,0.6,2,1,1
100,16,0.7,2,1,1
250,18,0.6,2,1,1
365,14,0.7,2,1,1
400,14,0.6,2,1,1

    Constriction Input File¶

    The constriction input file (w2_constriction.csv) allows the user to specify a maximum width between segments. This does not affect the width or volume of the segments; it only affects the right-hand side face width. Whereas an internal weir blocks all flow, a constriction allows for a reduced area and eliminates the need to insert a short segment of small width that can cause stability issues and a reduced time step.

    File Format¶

    The file has the following structure:

    1. Line 1: Comment
    2. Line 2: ON or OFF, number of constrictions
    3. Line 3: Comment
    4. Line 4+: Repeated for each constriction: segment number, maximum width in m

    Example¶

    Constriction input file
ON,2,,,
Segment # of constriction, Maximum width in m of constriction
15,50.0,
28,35.0,

    The W2 code looks for the file w2_constriction.csv and if it is found, the model reads the file and applies the specified maximum width to the right-hand side width between the specified two segments. This could be appropriate for bridge piers or other constrictions between two segments that might affect the flow between segments.

    Hypolimnetic Aeration Input File¶

    The hypolimnetic aeration input file (w2_aerate.npt) specifies parameters for the hypolimnetic aeration algorithm. The model reads this file if the AERATEC control is set to ON in the control file (w2_con.npt or w2_con.csv). This input file allows the user to:

    1. Add oxygen mass to specified model segments and layers
    2. Control the oxygen mass added by using oxygen probes located anywhere in the model domain
    3. Increase the mixing associated with the oxygen input

    This algorithm does not model the dynamics of a bubble diffuser. It allows the user to experiment with delivering oxygen at given rates to parts of the reservoir domain. This is useful for planning, since it allows the user to determine the amount of oxygen required to meet a water quality target.

    File Format¶

    Parameter Values Description
    AERATION ON/OFF Turn on or off aeration/mixing
    NAER Integer (1 to max) Number of aerators. Each aerator can also represent the same aerator turned ON/OFF at different times.
    OUTPUTFILE Filename Output file name
    SEG# Integer Segment number where the aerator is located
    KTOP# Integer Top layer number of the aerator (one aerator can span more than one layer)
    KBOT# Integer Bottom layer number of the aerator
    MASSRT Real Rate of mass injection of O₂ (kg O₂ day⁻¹)
    TIMON Real (Julian day) Start date for the aerator
    TIMOFF Real (Julian day) End date for the aerator
    DZFACT Real (1 to 100) Factor to multiply the vertical mixing coefficient. A value of 1 uses the model-predicted vertical mixing coefficient. A value of 100 increases mixing by 100x.
    O2OFF Real (mg L⁻¹) DO concentration at the probe location at which the aerator turns OFF
    O2ON Real (mg L⁻¹) DO concentration at the probe location at which the aerator turns ON
    iprb Integer Segment number of the DO probe for controlling the aerator
    kprb Integer Layer number of the DO probe for controlling the aerator

    Mixing Factor

    Set DZFACT = 1 if the aerator does not mix the water column. If DZFACT > 1, the vertical eddy diffusivity (DZ) is increased as a result of additional vertical mixing induced by the aerator. Use a sensitivity analysis to evaluate the effect of increased vertical mixing on aerator efficiency.

    Example¶

    CONTROL FILE FOR Hypolimnetic aeration
    NAER    OUTPUTFILE          # of aerators
       1    aeration.opt
                                kg O2/d
  SEG#  KTOP#  KBOT#  MASSRT   TIMON  TIMOFF  DZFACT   O2OFF    O2ON    iprb    kprb
    31     25     30  1000.      1.0   125.0      5.    12.5    11.0      31      30

    SYSTDG Input Files¶

    SYSTDG (System Total Dissolved Gas) is an empirical correlation for TDG at spillways at hydropower facilities. A detailed description of this algorithm is provided in the User Manual Part 2 under Dissolved Oxygen/Dam Reaeration.

    W2 SYSTDG Control File¶

    The SYSTDG control file, w2_systdg.npt, contains parameters that allow the user to configure the characteristics of spillways, powerhouses, and fish passage within the model. The control file is set up as a text file in fixed format with 8-digit numbers or characters. Each card is required although there may be either zero or no values associated with the card. The code looks for the file w2_systdg.npt. If it exists, it is read.

    TITLE C¶

    The first input is a title containing 10 title cards for each simulation that can be used to identify various types of output. Each line may contain up to 72 characters of text.

    SYSTDGC Card¶
    SYSTDGC  SYSTDG   N2BND   DOBND   TDGTA
              ON      ON      ON     OFF
    Variable Description
    SYSTDG ON/OFF control to allow calculation of tailrace TDG production using SYSTDG equations
    N2BND ON/OFF control to specify N₂ boundary conditions with percentage of saturation. If ON, N₂ in boundary conditions must be specified as percentage of saturation.
    DOBND ON/OFF control to specify DO boundary conditions with percentage of saturation. If ON, DO in boundary conditions must be specified as percentage of saturation.
    TDGTA ON/OFF control to allow performing spillway operations set by TDG targets. If ON, a TDG target control file (w2_tdgtarget.npt) must be provided.
    GATE GAS Card¶

    The next lines describe the gates used. The number of gates (NGT) described in the w2_con.npt (or w2_con.csv) is used to determine the number of lines to read.

    GATE GAS  GTTYPE    SPBC
SB1          SPB     1.0
SB2          SPB     1.0
RO1           RO     1.0
RO2           RO     1.0
POW1         POW     1.0
POW2         POW     1.0
    Variable Description
    GTTYPE Gate type: SPB (spillbay gate), POW (powerhouse gate), FLD (fish collector gate), RO (regulating outlet gate)
    SPBC Spill pattern specific coefficient in SYSTDG (only necessary if GTTYPE = SPB)

    For the individual gate, GASGTC in the control file must be set to ON in order to calculate the TDG production using SYSTDG equations.

    GATE Card¶
    GATE        FBE    TWCE  TWEMOD     TWE   TWETS  TDGLOC
SPB1       -1.0   135.0       0   160.0      ON     REL
    Variable Description
    FBE Forebay elevation (feet). If FBE > 0, two sets of coefficients (P1, P2, P3, P4) are required for the spillway TDG production equations.
    TWCE Tailwater channel elevation (feet)
    TWEMOD Integer parameter defining whether TWE will be recalculated (0 = user-defined, 1 = computed using twe = twe_obs * 0.934 + 4.94)
    TWE Averaged tailwater elevation (feet)
    TWETS ON/OFF logic parameter defining if a time series file with observed tailwater elevation is given
    TDGLOC Character parameter: SPB (spillbay) or REL (tailrace) for TDG calculated location
    GATE TDG Card¶
    GATE TDG  TDGEQ      P1      P2      P3      P4      P1      P2      P3      P4
SPB1          3     1.0     1.0   0.671    23.5   400.0     0.0  -0.020     0.0
RO            1  95.791 1.07193 178.852   -0.51
    Variable Description
    TDGEQ Equation number (1 to 5) for calculating spillway TDG production
    P1, P2, P3, P4 Spillway production coefficients (first set). If FBE > 0, a second set is also required.
    GATE ENT Card¶
    GATE ENT  TDGENT      E1      E2      E1      E2
SPB1          1   0.150     0.0     0.0     0.0
    Variable Description
    TDGENT Equation number (0, 1, 2, 3) for calculating powerhouse flow entrainment. Only spillway TDG is calculated if TDGENT = 0.
    E1, E2 Coefficients for the entrainment equation. If FBE > 0, two sets are required.
    TWETS FILE Card¶
    TWETS FILE...............................TWEFN...............................
SPB1     TWE1.npt
    Variable Description
    TWEFN File name for observed time series tailwater elevation (feet). Required if TWETSC = ON.

    TDG Target Control File¶

    The TDG target control file is named w2_TDGtarget.csv and must include all inputs required for performing spillway operation set by a TDG target. It contains parameters that allow the user to allocate spill flows into the powerhouse to reduce spillway TDG and meet user-specified TDG targets. The control file is set up as a comma-delimited format. Each row is required, although there may be either zero or no values associated with some parameters.

    TDGTRGET Card¶
    Variable Description
    TSFREQ Frequency (fraction of a day) at which the spill flow allocation calculations for a TDG target are updated
    TSCONV Convergence criterion for the iterative spill flow allocation solution, constrained to be 0.1 or less but nonzero
    TDGTRGET YEARLY Card¶
    Variable Description
    YEARLY ON/OFF: defines whether start and end dates for spill flow allocation should be repeated each year
    TSTR Start date (Julian day) for spill flow allocation calculations
    TEND End date (Julian day) for spill flow allocation calculations
    TTARGET Constant TDG target (%) to meet for that period of dates
    TSDYN ON/OFF: defines if a time series of TDG targets is used. If ON, the model overrides TTARGET and uses a user-specified time series from an external file.
    ITER Maximum number of flow allocation iterations allowed for numerical convergence
    PRIDYN ON/OFF: defines if priority of spill bays is dynamically set by their spill flow rates
    DYNGRP Number of priority groups to dynamically allocate spill flow
    PRIORITY Card¶
    Variable Description
    BAY# Priority setting for each spill bay. A value of -1 means the bay is not adjusted. Values >= 1 are interpreted as higher priorities with lower input values.
    MINFRACT Card¶
    Variable Description
    MINFRC# Minimum flow fraction (0 to 1) specifying the minimum fraction of total spill flow through that bay. If specified as a negative number, this input is interpreted as a minimum flow rate (m³ s⁻¹).
    MAXFLOW Card¶
    Variable Description
    PHMAX# Maximum available powerhouse capacity for spill flow allocation (m³ s⁻¹). A zero means that no criterion is specified.
    TARGET FILE Card¶
    Variable Description
    Filename If TSDYN is ON, this is the time series file of TDG targets. The file format is consistent with other W2 time series input files.
    Example TDG Target Time Series File¶
      JDAY      TDG
  1.000   105.0
 90.000   105.0
 91.000   115.0
181.000   115.0
182.000   115.0
366.000   125.0

    Graph Input File¶

    The file graph.npt is required only when using the fixed-format control file (w2_con.npt). When using the CSV control file (w2_con.csv), the graph.npt file is not needed because the equivalent information is included directly in the CSV control file tabs.

    The file controls output variable formats, multipliers, and plotting ranges. It contains three sections:

    1. Hydrodynamic variables (HFMT/HMULT): Each line specifies the Fortran output format (HFMT), output multiplier (HMULT), and minimum/maximum values for plotting. Hydrodynamic variables include water surface elevation, temperature, horizontal velocity, vertical velocity, and other physical variables.

    2. Active constituents (CMULT): Each line specifies the output format (FMTC), concentration multiplier (CMULT), and minimum/maximum values for each active constituent. The CMULT multiplier is applied to constituent concentrations before writing to output files (TSR, SNP, profile, contour, spreadsheet). For example, set CMULT = 1000.0 to convert from g m⁻³ to mg m⁻³ for nutrient values.

    3. Derived constituents (CDMULT): Each line specifies the output format (FMTCD), derived multiplier (CDMULT), and minimum/maximum values for each derived constituent. The CDMULT multiplier functions identically to CMULT but applies to derived variables.

    Deprecated Fields

    The minimum and maximum plotting fields in graph.npt were originally used by older post-processing tools. These fields are still read by the code but have limited use with current post-processing software. Set them to reasonable values or zero.

    Multiplier Usage

    The concentration multiplier CMULT is a conversion factor applied to the output. Set CMULT = 1000.0 to convert from g m⁻³ to mg m⁻³ for nutrient values. The multiplier is applied to TSR, SNP, and other output files, but is not applied to WDO output files.

    Tecplot Input File¶

    The file w2_tecplotbr.csv specifies which branches are output to the Tecplot contour plot file. It contains:

    1. Comment line
    2. Number of branches to output
    3. Comment line
    4. Branch numbers (comma separated)
    # of branches to output to TECPLOT
2
Put the branches you want to be output to TECPLOT on the next line separated by a comma
1,2

    Lake River Contour Plots Input File¶

    The lake_river_contour.csv input file specifies parameters for generating lake and river contour plots. The model senses the presence of this file and activates the feature automatically.

    File Structure¶

    The file is organized as follows:

    1. Control line: ON or OFF to enable or disable contour output
    2. Lake contour parameters:
      • Number of lake contours (maximum 20) and output format (1 or 2)
      • For each lake contour: segment number, start Julian day, and output frequency (days)
    3. River contour parameters:
      • Number of river contours (maximum 20) and output format (1 or 2)
      • For each river contour: start branch, end branch, start Julian day, and output frequency (days)

    Output Formats¶

    Two output formats are available:

    • Format 1: Writes a single output file per contour location containing elevation, distance, temperature, velocity, and all active constituents at each output time. This format is suitable for creating time-depth contour plots at a single segment (lake) or distance-depth contour plots along a branch (river).
    • Format 2: Writes separate output files for each variable, which can simplify post-processing for specific constituents.

    Example¶

    ON
2,1
15,1.0,0.5
30,1.0,0.5
1,1
1,2,1.0,1.0

    In this example, two lake contour locations (segments 15 and 30) and one river contour (branches 1 through 2) are specified, each starting at Julian day 1.0.

    Algae Vertical Migration Input File¶

    The w2_AlgaeMigration.csv input file describes parameters for the vertical migration model of Overman (2019). This file template is in the Excel input file as a tab named w2_AlgaeMigration. The parameter AVERTM is read in from the control file for each algae group. If AVERTM is ON for any group, this input file is read.

    File Structure¶

    The file contains the following lines (repeated for each algae group that migrates):

    Line Description
    AlgaeMigration: ON/OFF Turns algae migration ON or OFF for all algae groups. This supersedes the AVERTM control in the main control file.
    NumAlgaeGroups# of algae groups that migrate Number of algae groups that will migrate vertically, and debug output switch (1 = ON, 0 = OFF)
    NumMigrateIntervals Number of time intervals for algal migration (Models 1 and 2 only)
    JDAYStart Start Julian day of each migration interval
    JDAYend End Julian day of each migration interval
    ContinuumModel Migration model to use: 1 = velocity time, 2 = velocity time and space, 3 = density growth, 4 = density growth Visser
    Model 1 and 2 Parameters (Velocity-Based)¶
    Parameter Description
    Amplitude(m) Migration amplitude (m day⁻¹)
    PhaseShift(radians) Phase shift of the daily migration cycle
    CalibrationCoefficientExtinction Calibration coefficient (Model 2 only)
    DepthCalcOnOff(1-ON/0-OFF) Switch for depth calculation
    ExpDepth(m) Exponential depth parameter
    DepthLimitOnOff(1-ON/0-OFF) Switch to impose depth limit on migration
    DepthLimit(m) Depth limit (m)
    LossFraction Fraction of biomass lost at the depth limit
    Model 3 Parameters (Density Growth)¶
    Parameter Description
    ColonyRadius(m) Colony radius (m)
    MinColonyDen (kg m⁻³) Minimum colony density
    MaxColonyDen (kg m⁻³) Maximum colony density
    InitColonyDenSurf (kg m⁻³) Initial colony density at surface
    InitColonyDenBot (kg m⁻³) Initial colony density at bottom
    CompIrrad (W m⁻²) Compensation irradiance
    DecayTimeConstant (day⁻¹) Decay time constant
    DecayTimeStep (s) Decay time step
    CoeffDenIncr (kg m⁻³ s⁻¹) Coefficient for density increase
    CoeffDenDecr (kg m⁻³ s⁻¹) Coefficient for density decrease
    DepthLimitOnOff Depth limit switch
    DepthLimit(m) Depth limit (m)
    LossFraction Loss fraction at depth limit
    Model 4 Parameters (Density Growth Visser)¶

    Model 4 is the Visser density migration model using the equations described in Visser et al. (1997). These parameters describe colony density changes based on irradiance.

    Example File Format¶

    AlgaeMigration: ON/OFF
ON
NumAlgaeGroups# of algae groups that migrate,Debug Output(1-ON/0-OFF)
1,1
NumMigrateIntervals - # of intervals for algal migration (Models 1 and 2 only)
2
JDAYStart - start of each migration interval
100,465
JDAYend
250,610
ContinuumModel: 1 - velocity time, 2 - velocity time and space, 3 - density growth, 4 - density growth (Visser)
1
Amplitude,PhaseShift,CalibrationCoefficientExtinction,DepthCalcOnOff(1-ON/0-OFF),ExpDepth,DepthLimitOnOff(1-ON/0-OFF),DepthLimit,LossFraction
2,1.570796,0,,,,25,0.05
ColonyRadius,MinColonyDen,MaxColonyDen,InitColonyDenSurf,InitColonyDenBot,CompIrrad,DecayTimeConstant,DecayTimeStep,CoeffDenIncr,CoeffDenDecr,MinDenDecSurf,MinDenDecBot,DepthLimitOnOff,DepthLimit,LossFraction
5.00E-05,920,1085,980,920,5,1000,0.0115,0.005,0,30,0.1
AlgeGroup - repeat for each NumAlgeGroups

    Repeated Groups

    The set of parameters from NumMigrateIntervals through the model-specific parameters are repeated for each algae group that migrates vertically.

    Algae Toxin Input File¶

    The w2_Algae_Toxin.csv input file describes parameters for modeling algal toxin production from cyanobacteria. This file template is in the master Excel file under the tab w2_Algae_Toxin.csv. A description of the conceptual model for toxins is in Part 2 of the User Manual. This file is read when any of the toxin state variables are active.

    File Structure¶

    The first line of the file controls the overall toxin computation and debug output:

    Parameter Description
    TOXINCONTROL: ATOX ON/OFF for all algae toxins (turn on/off toxin production in the model)
    ATOX_DEBUG ON/OFF for debugging output (writes to algae_toxin_debug.csv)

    For each of the 4 toxin groups (microcystin, cylindrospermopsin, anatoxin-A, saxitoxin), the following parameters are required. Each CTP and CTB value is specified for each algae group (ALG1, ALG2, ALG3, etc.).

    Parameter Description
    CTP Fraction of algae concentration producing toxin. For each algal group, specify whether that group produces toxin as a fraction of its biomass. If CTP = 0, no toxin is produced by that algal group.
    CTB Ratio of intracellular toxin to dry weight biomass (mg toxin mg⁻¹ DW)
    CTR Release rate of intracellular toxin to extracellular toxin (day⁻¹)
    CTD Extracellular decay rate (day⁻¹)

    Typical Parameter Ranges¶

    The following ranges provide guidance for initial parameterization. See Garstecki (2021) for a comprehensive review of parameter ranges and calibration strategies for algal toxin modeling in CE-QUAL-W2.

    Parameter Microcystin (MC) Cylindrospermopsin (CYN) Anatoxin-A (ATX-A) Saxitoxin (STX)
    CTB (mg toxin mg⁻¹ DW) 0.0005 to 0.024 0.0005 to 0.007 0.001 to 0.01 0.001 to 0.004
    CTR (day⁻¹) Approx. 0 to 1 times excretion rate Approx. 1 to 2 times excretion rate Approx. 0 to 1 times excretion rate Approx. 0 to 1 times excretion rate
    CTD (day⁻¹) 0.05 to 0.2 0.01 to 0.06 0.05 to 0.2 0.01 to 0.07

    Reference

    Garstecki, T. (2021). Modeling cyanotoxin production in CE-QUAL-W2: parameter estimation and sensitivity analysis. See Part 2 of the User Manual for the complete conceptual model description.

    Example File Format¶

    TOXINCONTROL: ATOX (turn ON/OFF all algae toxins),ATOX_DEBUG (turn on debugging output),ON/OFF,DEBUG
ON,ON
MICROCYSTIN,,ALG1,ALG2,ALG3
CTP_MC, fraction of algae concentration producing MC,,0.5,0.25,0.3
CTB_MC, ratio of intracellular toxin to dry weight biomass (mg toxin/mg DW),,0.1,0.2,0.2
CTR_MC, release rate, day-1,,0.5
CTD_MC, extracellular decay, day-1,,0.1
CYLINDROSPERMOPSIN,,ALG1,ALG2,ALG3
CTP_CYN, fraction of algae concentration producing CYN,,1,0,0
CTB_CYN, ratio of intracellular toxin to dry weight biomass (mg toxin/mg DW),,0.25,0.2,0.2
CTR_CYN, release rate, day-1,,0.5
CTD_CYN, extracellular decay, day-1,,0.25
ANATOXIN-A,,ALG1,ALG2,ALG3
CTP_ATX, fraction of algae concentration producing ATX,,0.5,0.25,0.3
CTB_ATX, ratio of intracellular toxin to dry weight biomass (mg toxin/mg DW),,0.1,0.2,0.2
CTR_ATX, release rate, day-1,,0.5
CTD_ATX, extracellular decay, day-1,,0.1
SAXITOXIN,,ALG1,ALG2,ALG3
CTP_STX, fraction of algae concentration producing STX,,0.5,0.25,0.3
CTB_STX, ratio of intracellular toxin to dry weight biomass (mg toxin/mg DW),,0.1,0.2,0.2
CTR_STX, release rate, day-1,,0.5
CTD_STX, extracellular decay, day-1,,0.1

    Column A

    Column A in the Excel template is not written to the w2_Algae_Toxin.csv input file. It is used only for descriptive labels in the spreadsheet.

    Dissolved Gas Added to Turbine Input File¶

    This file is a time series file of Julian day and a fraction multiplied by the incoming turbine dissolved oxygen. This file is assumed to be a step function input. The file is either for a spillway (w2_spX_DO.csv, where X is the spillway number) or for a gate (w2_gtX_DO.csv, where X is the gate number).

    The fraction will usually be greater than or equal to 1.0. If it is 1.0, the inflow oxygen is unchanged. If it is 1.1, the inflow oxygen is increased by 10%. Other parameters for the spillway or gate dictate whether the outflow oxygen is limited or not by 100% saturation.

    The file has the following format: the first 3 lines are ignored and then there is a time series of Julian day and fraction.

    Field Variable Description
    1 JDAY Julian day
    2 FRAC Fraction multiplied by incoming dissolved oxygen (DO)

    Example¶

    Input fraction for gate1
Dynamic turbine venting
JDAY,FRAC
1,1
210.5,1.1
300,1
365,1

    Automatic Port Selection¶

    The CE-QUAL-W2 model reads the file w2_selective.npt if the SELECTC control is set to ON or USGS in the control file (w2_con.npt or w2_con.csv). This input file allows the model user to:

    1. Automatically choose a withdrawal port elevation based on meeting required temperature targets
    2. Print temperature of individual outlets rather than combining them together
    3. Print the volume of the reservoir at specified temperature targets

    Note

    If DYNSTRUC is set to ON in the control file, the port selection in this file is ignored when there is not an active rule. When a rule is inactive, the input file of elevations (when DYNSTRUC=ON) will control the centerline of the structure.

    SELECTC = ON¶

    When SELECTC='ON', the input file is named w2_selective.npt. There are three independent sections: (1) adjust an existing structure to meet a temperature target, (2) use two structures to blend water to meet a temperature target, and (3) print out temperature volumes in the reservoir at specified temperatures.

    Output Frequency¶
    Selective input control file
Temperature outlet control - frequency of output for temperature
OUT FREQ TFRQTMP
        0.02083

    TFRQTMP (Real F8.0) is the Julian day frequency of the output for the temperature of each structure outlet. The value must be in columns 9-16 in Fortran F fixed format. A value of 0.02083 Julian day corresponds to every 30 minutes.

    Dynstr1 Card¶
    DYNSTR1  CONTROL    NUM    FREQ
             ON       2    0.50
    Variable Format Description
    CONTROL Character A8 ON or OFF: controls whether the algorithm is used
    NUM Integer I8 Number of selective withdrawal structures. Each subsequent line defines one structure
    FREQ Real F8.0 Julian day frequency for checking the temperature criteria. Set to 1.0 to check daily, 0.5 for every 12 hours
    Dynstr2 Card¶
    DYNSTR2  ST/WD    JB  JS/NW  YEARLY   TSTR   TEND   TEMP  NELEV  ELEV1  ELEV2
1           ST     1      1      ON   010.   045.   15.0      2  210.   190.
2           ST     2      1      ON   180.   300.   20.0      1   95.0
    Variable Format Description
    ST/WD Character A8 ST for a structure, WD for a withdrawal
    JB Integer I8 Branch number of the structure. Ignored for a withdrawal
    JS/NW Integer I8 Outlet number (if ST/WD='ST') or withdrawal number (if ST/WD='WD')
    YEARLY Character A8 ON or OFF. If ON, Julian days TSTR and TEND are applied yearly (must be 1--366). If OFF, applied only once
    TSTR Real F8.0 Beginning Julian day for starting selective withdrawal
    TEND Real F8.0 Ending Julian day for ending selective withdrawal. After TEND, the outlet elevation reverts to the control file value
    TEMP Real F8.0 Temperature criterion in °C for lowering or raising the structure elevation
    NELEV Integer I8 Number of selective withdrawal elevations (up to 100)
    ELEV1, ELEV2, ... Real Elevations in m of the selective withdrawal structures, ordered from high to low

    The model raises the elevation if the temperature criterion TEMP is above the actual temperature, or lowers the elevation if the actual temperature is above TEMP.

    Monitor Card¶
    MONITOR LOC ISEG    ELEV  DYNSEL
1            32        0     OFF
2            43        1     OFF
    Variable Format Description
    ISEG Integer I8 Segment number for temperature monitoring. If < 0, the absolute value must equal the withdrawal number segment in the WITH OUT card, and all withdrawals at that segment are combined. If = 0, uses the mixed outlet temperature of the structure/withdrawal. If > 0, uses the segment number as the monitoring point
    ELEV Real F8.0 Elevation for temperature monitoring. A negative number indicates a layer number. If ISEG=0 or ISEG<0, this is ignored
    DYNSEL Character A8 ON or OFF. If ON, a time-varying temperature criterion file named dynselectiveX.npt (where X is the structure number) is read

    When DYNSEL is ON, the time-varying temperature file has the following format:

    Dynamic selective input temperature input file 'dynselective1.npt'

    JDAY    TEMP
   1.000    10.0
  50.000    12.0
 150.000    15.0
 200.000    20.0
 265.000    15.0
 365.000    10.0

    This time series is treated as a step function input with no linear interpolation between successive values. If the file begins with a $ character, it is read as comma-delimited CSV format.

    Auto Elevcontrol Card¶
    AUTO ELEVCONTROL
1              ON
2             OFF

    ELEVCONTROL (Character A8) is either ON or OFF. When ON, the elevation of the selective withdrawal structure is reduced as the water level lowers, ensuring the water surface is always above the outlet elevation. This card is repeated for each outlet (number defined by NUM in the DYNSTR1 card).

    Split1 Card¶
    SPLIT1    CNTR     NUM
            ON       1
    Variable Format Description
    CNTR Character A8 ON or OFF: activates the flow-splitting algorithm
    NUM Integer I8 Number of outlet pairs to consider. Outlets must be from the same branch and defined in the control file
    Split2 Card¶
    SPLIT2  ST/WD    JB  YEARLY   TSTR   TEND  TTARGET  NOUTS JS1/NW1 JS2/NW2  ELCONT DYNSPLT
1          ST     2      ON   010.   045.     12.       2       2       3     OFF      ON
    Variable Format Description
    ST/WD Character A8 ST for a structure, WD for a withdrawal
    JB Integer I8 Branch number. Ignored for a withdrawal
    YEARLY Character A8 ON or OFF: apply TSTR and TEND yearly
    TSTR Real F8.0 Beginning Julian day for flow splitting
    TEND Real F8.0 Ending Julian day for flow splitting
    TTARGET Real F8.0 Temperature target (°C) at the branch outlet centerline
    NOUTS Integer I8 Number of outlets to apportion flows (current limit is 2)
    JS1/NW1 Integer I8 Structure or withdrawal number for the upper outlet
    JS2/NW2 Integer I8 Structure or withdrawal number for the lower outlet
    ELCONT Character A8 ON or OFF. If ON, the top outlet elevation follows the water surface when it drops below the outlet centerline
    DYNSPLT Character A8 ON or OFF. If ON, a time-varying temperature target file (dynselectiveX_splt.npt) is used instead of TTARGET

    The decision rules for apportioning flows between two outlets are:

    Rule Condition
    1 If T_JS1 > T_target and T_JS2 > T_target, take all flow from lower outlet (JS2)
    2 If T_JS1 < T_target and T_JS2 < T_target, take all flow from upper outlet (JS1)
    3 If T_JS1 > T_target and T_JS2 < T_target, apportion flow based on flow balance equation: Q_JS1 = Q_sum(T_target - T_JS2) / (T_JS1 - T_JS2) and Q_JS2 = Q_sum - Q_JS1
    4 If water elevation is below outlet elevation for upper outlet (JS1), take all flow from lower outlet (JS2)
    Thresh1 and Thresh2 Cards¶
    THRESH1  TEMPN
             2

THRESH2  TCRTWB1  TCRTWB2  TCRTWB3  TCRTWB4  TCRTWB5
1           10.0     10.0                                ! 52 deg. F
2           12.5     12.5                                ! 60 deg. F
    Variable Format Description
    TEMPN Integer I8 Number of temperature criteria for each waterbody
    TCRTWBx Real F8.0 Temperature threshold (°C) for each waterbody. The model outputs the volume of the reservoir at or below each threshold

    SELECTC = USGS¶

    When SELECTC='USGS', the input file is also named w2_selective.npt but has a different format supporting the USGS blending algorithm. This algorithm supports up to 10 outlets in a blending group. See Rounds and Buccola (2015) for details on the algorithm and example problems.

    Two new variables were added to the SPLIT1 input card:

    Variable Description
    TSFREQ Frequency (fraction of a day) at which blending calculations are updated
    TSCONV Convergence criterion for the iterative blending solution (fraction of total flow assigned to first-priority outlets; should be 0.1 or less, default 0.005)

    Additional input cards for the USGS algorithm include:

    Card Variable(s) Description
    SPLIT2 TSDYN ON or OFF: use time-varying temperature targets from dynsplit_selectiveX.npt
    SPLIT2 TSSHARE ON or OFF: share releases among set-2 outlets (ON) or choose the best single outlet (OFF)
    SPLITOUT JS1/NW1...JS10/NW10 Structure or outlet numbers (up to 10)
    DEPTH DEPTH1...DEPTH10 Nonzero value makes outlet a floating outlet at that depth below the water surface
    MINFRAC MINFRC1...MINFRC10 Minimum flow fraction (0--1) for each outlet. Negative values are interpreted as minimum flow rates in m³/s
    PRIORITY PRIOR1...PRIOR10 Integer priority setting. A value of -1 means the outlet is not blended but its temperature effect is accounted for. Values of 0 or greater are blending priorities (lower values = higher priority)
    MINHEAD MINHD1...MINHD10 Minimum head (depth in m) required for the outlet to be used
    MAXHEAD MAXHD1...MAXHD10 Maximum head (depth in m). If centerline is deeper than this, the outlet is not used
    MAXFLOW MAXFLO1...MAXFLO10 Maximum flow in m³/s for each blended outlet

    Example w2_selective.npt file for SELECTC='USGS':

    Selective input control file for SELECTC='USGS'
Temperature outlet control - frequency of output for temperature
OUT FREQ TFRQTMP
        0.125
Structure outlet control based on time and temperature and branch

1           ST     1      1      ON    1.0  151.0   10.0      2  340.   330.

MONITOR LOC ISEG    ELEV  DYNCEL
1              0    -185     OFF

            ON       1   0.125   0.005

2          15.55    15.55

    Error Trapping

    Error trapping for the USGS blending routine checks the following conditions:

    • At least 2 and no more than 10 outlets must be specified for NOUTS in each blending group on the SPLIT2 input card
    • Any single outlet can only be specified once in each blending group (SPLITOUT input card)
    • Any single outlet can only be specified in one blending group or temperature control device at a time (start and end dates for each group are checked)
    • Integer priority specifications must be -1 or greater (PRIORITY input card)

    Environmental Performance Criteria¶

    This section describes the environmental performance criteria input file, which allows the model user to assess model predictions of state variables and how they can be affected by management changes. The model reads the file w2_envirprf.npt if the ENVIRPC control is set to ON in the control file (w2_con.npt or w2_con.csv).

    The analysis computes a histogram of the fraction of time and volume during a model run where the selected water quality state variable falls between specified intervals. This input file allows the model user to:

    1. Compute fraction of the reservoir volume and time associated with velocity, temperature, and depth levels
    2. Compute fraction of the reservoir volume and time associated with model state variables for water quality
    3. Compute fraction of the reservoir volume and time associated with model-derived variables for water quality

    The analysis produces the following output files (when all options are turned on):

    Output File Contents
    envrprf_t.csv Temperature histogram
    envrprf_v.csv Velocity histogram
    envrprf_depth.csv Depth histogram
    envrprf_c.csv Concentration histogram for state variables
    envrprf_cd.csv Concentration histogram for derived variables

    The input file w2_envirprf.npt is CSV (comma delimited) or fixed format. The model checks the file to determine which format is being used.

    File Format¶

    ENVIRONMENTAL PERFORMANCE CRITERIA CE-QUAL-W2 MODEL
IINTVLS  INTVLS SELECTC   JDAY1   JDAY2   ISTR1   IEND1   ISTR2   IEND2
1            20     OFF   100.    150.        5      25
    Header and Interval Settings¶

    The first two lines are ignored and serve as titles.

    Variable Format Description
    IINTVLS Integer I1 Number of segment intervals for computing histograms (1--9). Setting this to 1 uses all active segments. Each additional interval requires an ISTR and IEND pair
    INTVLS Integer I8 Number of intervals (bins) for the output histogram
    SELECTC Character A3 ON or OFF. If OFF, all days and all active model cells are used. If ON, JDAY1, JDAY2, ISTR1, and IEND1 define the range
    JDAY1 Real F8.0 Start Julian day for the analysis. Use negative values for multi-year runs (e.g., -213 for August start every year)
    JDAY2 Real F8.0 End Julian day for the analysis
    ISTR1 Integer I8 First segment number for the analysis
    IEND1 Integer I8 Last segment number for the analysis
    ISTR2, IEND2 Integer I8 Additional segment interval pairs (up to 9 intervals)
    Velocity, Temperature, and Depth Settings¶
    VELOCTY   VINCR   VTOP   TEMPC   TINCR   TTOP  DEPTHC   DINCR   DTOP
    OFF   0.050   0.50      ON     1.0   30.0     OFF     4.0   110.0
    Variable Format Description
    VELOCTY Character A8 ON or OFF: compute a histogram of velocities
    VINCR Real F8.0 Velocity increment in m/s
    VTOP Real F8.0 Maximum velocity in the histogram
    TEMPC Character A8 ON or OFF: compute a histogram of temperatures
    TINCR Real F8.0 Temperature increment in °C for histogram bins
    TTOP Real F8.0 Maximum temperature in °C for the histogram output
    DEPTHC Character A8 ON or OFF: compute a histogram of depths in m
    DINCR Real F8.0 Depth increment in m for histogram bins
    DTOP Real F8.0 Maximum depth in m for the histogram output
    Constituent State Variables¶
    CST       ACTIVE INTSCLE TOPLMIT
TDS          OFF   1.00   20.00
WaterAge      ON   1.00   20.00
Gen2          ON   1.00   20.00
GEN3          ON   1.00   20.00
ISS1          ON   1.00   20.00
PO4          OFF   1.00   20.00
NH4          OFF   1.00   20.00
...
DO            ON   0.50   10.00         ! 26
...

    Each constituent state variable has the following fields:

    Variable Format Description
    ACTIVE Character A8 ON or OFF: include this constituent in the histogram analysis
    INTSCLE Real F8.0 Interval scale (increment) for histogram bins. For DO, a value of 0.5 gives 0.5 mg/L bins
    TOPLMIT Real F8.0 Maximum value for the histogram. For DO with INTSCLE=0.5 and TOPLMIT=10.0, there are 20 bins

    The constituent list follows the standard CE-QUAL-W2 constituent order. The ! character can be used for inline comments.

    Derived Variables¶
    CST DERI  ACTIVE INTSCLE TOPLMIT
DOC          OFF   1.00   20.00
POC          OFF   1.00   20.00
TOC          OFF   1.00   20.00
DON          OFF   1.00   20.00
...
SECCHI       OFF   1.00   20.00

    The derived variables section has the same format as the state variables section. Derived variables include DOC, POC, TOC, DON, PON, TON, TKN, TN, DOP, POP, TOP, TP, APR, CHLA, ATOT, %DO, TDG, Turbidity, TSS, TISS, CBOD, pH, CO₂, HCO₃, CO₃, and SECCHI.

    Setting Up Environmental Performance Criteria

    When setting up the environmental performance criteria, focus on constituents that are relevant to your management questions. Turn ACTIVE to ON only for variables of interest to minimize output file sizes. Adjust INTSCLE and TOPLMIT to provide appropriate histogram resolution for each variable.

    The typical output file is comma delimited and can be opened directly in Excel. The first series of output lines form a histogram of the fraction of volume and time that the variable was in the specified interval. At the end of the column there is a sum of the fractions (which should sum to 1.0) and the last line is the temporal and volume-weighted average over the entire model domain and simulation time period.

    Output Files¶

    Snapshot File¶

    The snapshot file was designed for output to a hard copy printer and contains useful information that can be utilized during model application. The file can be read using any text editor. It is not recommended for graphing; other output files are more suitable for plotting model predictions.

    The snapshot file is controlled by the SNP PRINT, SNP DATE, SNP FREQ, and SNP SEG cards. The output is updated to the file based on the update dates [SNPD] and frequency [SNPF] specified in the control file. The file contains the following sections for each output time.

    Title Cards¶

    The first lines of output contain the information specified in the Title Card for identifying the run plus the date and time that the run occurred. This information will appear every time output is updated to the file. Following the title cards is information related to the progress of the run including the output date and timestep information.

    Example:

    CE-QUAL-W2  V3.1
 Burnsville Reservoir - March 15 through December 11, 1992
 Density placed inflow, point sink outflows
 Default hydraulic coefficients
 Default light absorption/extinction coefficients
 Default kinetic coefficients
 Temperature and water quality simulation
 Run 8
 Testing sensitivity to wind
 Wind sheltering set to 0.75
 Jim Stiles and Vince Marchese, USACE Huntington District
 Model run at 16:58:03 on 07/22/02

    Time Parameters¶

    Field Variable Description
    Gregorian date GDAY Calendar date
    Julian date JDAY Julian day and hours
    Elapsed time ELTMJD Days and hours since simulation start
    Timestep DLT Current timestep (s) and location (KLOC, ILOC)
    Minimum timestep MINDLT Minimum timestep to date (s), date, and location
    Average timestep DLTAV Average timestep (s)
    Number of iterations NIT Total iterations to date
    Number of violations NV Number of timestep violations

    Example:

    Time Parameters
  Gregorian date     [GDAY] =                March 15, 1992
  Julian date        [JDAY] =       75 days 12.00 hours
  Timestep            [DLT] =          356 sec
    at location  [KLOC,ILOC] = (26,47)
  Average timestep  [DLTAV] =          600 sec
  Number of iterations [NIT] =           72
  Number of violations  [NV] =            5

    Meteorological Parameters¶

    The next information includes meteorological parameters used at the current timestep. This can be useful when trying to understand temperature simulations. Running sensitivity analyses on meteorological forcing can show that the model will reproduce observed data if the forcing values are more accurate.

    Example:

    Meteorological Parameters
  Input
    Air temperature        [TAIR]  =      0.55 °C
    Dewpoint temperature   [TDEW]  =     -8.59 °C
    Wind direction          [PHI]  =      4.65 rad
    Cloud cover           [CLOUD]  =      4.53
  Calculated
    Equilibrium temperature  [ET]  =      0.00 °C
    Surface heat exchange  [CSHE]  = 0.00E+00 m/sec
    Short wave radiation    [SRO]  = 0.14E-03 °C m/sec

    Selective Withdrawal and Inflow/Outflow Parameters¶

    Inflows, inflow placement, and inflow temperatures are output to provide information about what the model is seeing at all inflow boundaries during the current timestep. All outflows are also printed including individual structure outflows, the layer-by-layer outflow computed from the selective withdrawal algorithm, and the total outflow. The final information includes inflow constituent concentrations for all boundary inflows.

    Balances¶

    Water balance, energy balance, and mass balance results for each branch and waterbody, including:

    • Spatial and temporal change in volume/energy/mass
    • Error and percent error between the two

    Balance Checks

    Volume and mass balance errors should be on the order of 10⁻⁶ to 10⁻¹³ percent. Larger errors may indicate code issues or numerical instability.

    Geometry¶

    The water surface layer number, elevation at the downstream segment, and the current upstream segment number for each branch are output.

    Water Surface¶

    The water surface elevation and the water surface deviation from the top of the water surface layer number are output next. The water surface deviation uses the oceanographic convention in which the deviation downwards from the top of the surface layer is positive. The output includes information only for the segments specified on the Snapshot Segments card.

    Temperature/Water Quality¶

    The last information available from the snapshot file covers hydrodynamics and water quality. The user controls which variables are included in the output. Include only variables of interest since the file can become quite large. Title cards are printed on each new page for ease in identifying the simulation.

    Sediment Diagenesis Output Files¶

    Output files from the sediment diagenesis routine are CSV files for graphing. Key output files include:

    File Description
    DiagenesisLogFile.opt Layer addition information for bed consolidation
    DiagBubbleReleaseSummary.csv Summary of bubble release predictions
    DiagBubbles.csv Bubble radius, gas concentrations, crack status
    Diagenesis_SD_JCH4.csv Dissolved methane sediment fluxes
    Diagenesis_SD_JNH4.csv Sediment N flux
    Diagenesis_SD_JNO3.csv Sediment NO₃ flux
    Diagenesis_SD_JPO4.csv Sediment PO₄ flux
    Diagenesis_SOD.csv Sediment oxygen demand (CSOD+NCSOD)
    Diagenesis_NSOD.csv Nitrogenous SOD
    Diagenesis_CSOD.csv Carbonaceous SOD
    Diagenesis_JC.csv Sediment C flux from water column
    Diagenesis_JN.csv Sediment N flux from water column
    Diagenesis_JP.csv Sediment P flux from water column
    Diagenesis_TemperatureAerobicLayer.csv Temperature in aerobic layer
    Diagenesis_TemperatureAnaerobicLayer.csv Temperature in anaerobic layer
    Diagenesis_POCG1.csv Labile POC concentrations
    Diagenesis_POCG2.csv Refractory POC concentrations
    Diagenesis_PONG1.csv Labile PON concentrations
    Diagenesis_PONG2.csv Refractory PON concentrations
    Diagenesis_POPG1.csv Labile POP concentrations
    Diagenesis_POPG2.csv Refractory POP concentrations

    Additional output files include porewater concentrations (NO₃, NH₃, PO₄, SO₄, Fe, Mn, CH₄ for aerobic and anaerobic layers), transfer velocities, and bubble model outputs.

    Time Series¶

    A time series history can be output for any number of cells in the computational grid with output for each cell written to a separate file. Output is suitable for import into a spreadsheet program for analysis and plotting and is in comma-delimited format. If the TSR filename is set to a file suffix csv, then all TSR files will have that suffix and will open in Excel automatically. The model takes the name of the time series file specified on the TIME SERIES PLOT FILENAME card and appends _x to the filename where x = 1, 2, 3, etc., depending upon whether the cell is the first, second, third, etc. one specified on the TIME SERIES SEGMENT card.

    The time series file includes the following information:

    • Julian date and current time step (s)
    • Water surface elevation for the cell's segment location (m)
    • Temperature (°C) at the layer and segment specified
    • Velocity (m s⁻¹) at the layer and segment specified
    • Total flow rate through the entire segment (m³ s⁻¹), vertically integrated flow through the segment
    • Net short wave solar radiation incident on the water surface (W m⁻²; reflection is not included, hence it would be the internally computed SRO which includes reflection x 1.06)
    • Light extinction coefficient in m⁻¹ at the layer and segment specified
    • Depth from water surface to channel bottom (m)
    • Surface width (m)
    • Shade (shade factor multiplied by SRON; if SHADE = 1, no shade; if SHADE = 0, no short wave solar reaches the water surface)
    • Net radiation at surface of segment (W m⁻²), short wave solar net at surface (W m⁻²), long wave radiation net in at surface (W m⁻²), back radiation at surface (W m⁻²), evaporative heat flux at surface (W m⁻²), conductive heat flux at surface (W m⁻²)
    • Reaeration coefficient (day⁻¹) predicted by the model at the segment of the TSR file
    • Active constituent concentrations
    • Derived constituent concentrations
    • Instantaneous kinetic flux rates in kg day⁻¹
    • Instantaneous algal growth rate limitation fractions for P, N, and light [0 to 1] for each algal group
    • If LIMC is turned on under the CST COMP card, instantaneous epiphyton growth rate limitation fractions for P, N, and light [0 to 1] for each epiphyton group

    If the first-order sediment model is ON, the TSR file will also output SED (total organic matter in sediments in g m⁻³), SEDN (Organic N in sediments in g m⁻³), SEDP (organic P in sediments in g m⁻³), and SEDC (organic C in sediments in g m⁻³). The mass of organic matter, N, P, and C is normalized by the volume of the model cell in contact with the sediment layer.

    Equilibrium Temperature Approach

    If SLHTC is set to ET for equilibrium temperature approach, then the radiation fluxes will all be set to zero.

    Auto-Port Selection Output Files¶

    The auto-port selection routine writes two types of output files.

    Volume at Specified Temperatures (Volume_wbX.opt)¶

    The volume at specified temperature files are named Volume_wbX.opt, where X is the waterbody number. Each file contains a time series of Julian day, total volume (m³), and volumes below the temperature thresholds defined by TEMPCRIT in the w2_selective.npt control file. The number of volume columns corresponds to the number of temperature thresholds specified.

    jday     Volume       Volcrit       Volcrit
22919.05 0.1650E+10  0.1565E+10  0.1650E+10
22919.09 0.1650E+10  0.1563E+10  0.1650E+10
22919.13 0.1650E+10  0.1561E+10  0.1650E+10
22919.17 0.1650E+10  0.1560E+10  0.1650E+10
22919.21 0.1650E+10  0.1557E+10  0.1650E+10

    Outlet Temperatures and Elevations (wd_out.opt and str_brX.csv)¶

    When SELECTC = ON, the model prints a separate time series of outlet temperatures and flows for each individual outlet, in addition to the existing CE-QUAL-W2 combined temperature time series. The outlet files are named str_brX.csv where X is the branch number. A similar file is written for withdrawals in wd_out.opt.

    The format includes columns for Julian day, temperature for each structure, flow for each structure, and elevation for each structure.

    Branch:          1  # of structures:      3  outlet temperatures
    JDAY       T(C)      T(C)      T(C)    Q(m3/s)   Q(m3/s)   Q(m3/s)   ELEVCL    ELEVCL    ELEVCL
22920.000      0.00      9.52      7.20      0.00     69.27     17.91   300.23    280.42    173.74
22921.000      0.00      9.76      7.20      0.00     68.36     17.57   300.23    280.42    173.74
22922.000      0.00      9.81      7.20      0.00     67.46     17.23   300.23    280.42    173.74

    Fish Habitat Output Files¶

    There are several output files defined in the fish habitat input file: one for fish habitat, volume-weighted averages for an entire segment over all vertical layers, surface-weighted averages over a fixed number of vertical layers, and accumulation of sediments in the first-order sediment model.

    Fish Habitat¶

    The fish habitat zones are defined in the w2_habitat.npt input file. The output file reprints the original criteria and then, at the output frequency of the TSR FREQ card, outputs Julian day, percent habitat volume for species 1, actual habitat volume in m³ for species 1, and then repeats this for each species.

    Fish habitat analysis: CE-QUAL-W2 model results

 Species, Temperature minimum, Temperature maximum, Dissolved oxygen minimum
RainbowTrout,             0.00,   18.00,    5.00
StripedBass,             10.00,   24.00,    5.00
Walleye,                 12.00,   24.00,    5.00
WhiteBass,                0.00,   28.00,    3.00
SmallmouthBass,           0.00,   29.00,    4.00
SpottedBass,              0.00,   24.40,    6.00

JDAY,%VOL-RainbowTrout,HAB-VOL(m3)-RainbowTrout,%VOL-StripedBass,...
   61.532,   100.00,  0.1170E+09,     0.00,  0.0000E+00,...

    The model also writes output for each branch and waterbody if HABTATC is ON. For each waterbody, the model writes fish_habitat_wbX.csv where X is the waterbody number. For each branch, the model writes fish_habitat_brX.csv where X is the branch number. These files allow the model user to explore fish habitat in smaller sections of the waterbody.

    Volume-Weighted and Surface-Weighted Averages¶

    Volume-weighted averages are computed at specified segments. The output includes Julian day and the following parameters at each specified segment: PO₄, NH₄, NO₃, DO, TP, Chl-a, and Gamma (an index variable).

    Surface-weighted averages are similar but computed over a specified number of layers from the water surface. The format matches the fish habitat output file, with blank lines at the beginning.

    Organic Matter Accumulation¶

    The organic matter accumulation output file contains comma-delimited entries for each segment and time of output. The values are grams of organic matter at the bottom of each layer, summed for each segment.

    Preprocessor¶

    The preprocessor produces several output files: a file that echoes all control file inputs along with additional information (pre.opt), a warning file that alerts the user to potential problems with inputs (pre.wrn), and an error file (pre.err) that identifies serious problems in the input data that may prevent the model from running or running correctly. Run the preprocessor routinely before a model simulation.

    In the Windows version, the user can supply a command-line working directory argument so that the preprocessor executable does not need to be copied into every model directory:

    preW2_ivf.exe "C:\scott\w2workshop\2009 workshop\waterqual\problem3"

    Output (pre.opt)¶

    The title cards are echoed at the beginning of the output followed by a complete echoing of all control file input. A description of the input, the Fortran variable name used in the control file, and the value of the variable are output. The sequence of output closely follows the sequence of input in the control file, but in some cases it is different as certain types of output are more logically grouped together. The pre.opt file contains the following major sections:

    Time Control and Simulation Parameters. The first section includes variables affecting the time of simulation and the timestep, followed by initial conditions and variables that control calculations in the model.

    Time Control
  Starting time (Julian day) [TMSTRT] =   75.00
  Ending time (Julian day)   [TMEND]  =  320.00
  Year                       [YEAR]   =   1992
  # Timestep intervals       [NDLT]   =      1
  Minimum timestep (sec)     [DLTMIN] =    1.0
  Maximum timestep (sec)     [DLTMAX] = 1800.0
  Fraction of timestep       [DLTF]   =   0.85

    Input Interpolations and Meteorological Parameters. Controls for all input interpolation are output, followed by meteorological parameters and a statistical summary of all meteorological variables in the input file.

    Meteorological Data Input Summary
    Parameter  Waterbody  Average Value  Maximum   Minimum
    TAIR(C)        1        26.258       37.300    16.000
    TDEW(C)        1        22.850       29.000     8.500
    WIND(m/s)      1         0.592       11.490     0.000

    Transport, Hydraulic, and Ice Cover Parameters. Variables affecting the transport solution, hydraulics, and ice cover.

    Output Controls. Snapshot, screen, fluxes, vector plot, profile plot, spreadsheet plot, contour plot, time series, and restart settings with their dates and frequencies.

    Inflow/Outflow Information. Selective withdrawal structure information, tributary details, withdrawal details, distributed tributary settings, and all input and output filenames.

    Constituent Parameters. State variable computation, initial concentration, flux, and printout settings; boundary condition constituent controls; derived variable computation controls; kinetic rate constants; stoichiometric relationships; light attenuation coefficients; half-saturation constants.

    Inflow Constituent Statistics. Summary statistics (average, maximum, minimum) for inflow temperatures and constituent concentrations.

    Water Balance Summary. Average and maximum total inflows and outflows for each waterbody and branch.

    Geometry. All information from the control and bathymetry file along with a computed area-volume-elevation table that includes average width, depth, and theoretical hydraulic residence time. The surface layer is indicated by KT next to the layer number.

    Branch 1 Volume-Area-Elevation Table

Layer  Elevation  Area        Volume      Active Cells  Average depth  Average width
       (m)        (1.0E6 m^2) (1.0E6 m^3)               (m)            (m)
  2    251.17     5.171       58.316       630           11.3           262.48
 23 KT 238.36     2.413        8.844       216            3.7           122.50

    Initial Conditions. Initial conditions for the water surface deviation, temperature, and constituent concentrations. Check these to ensure correctness, particularly when using vertical and/or longitudinal profile files.

    Warning Messages (pre.wrn)¶

    The preprocessor does an extensive check of inputs to determine if inputs "make sense." Because the model is flexible, certain inputs are not necessarily errors but should be flagged so the user can verify their intent. When kinetic rates differ from valid ranges or a specific value that should not normally be changed, a warning message is generated.

    All warning messages include a concise statement in English showing what the problem is. The Fortran variable name is also included along with its value.

    Example:

    Epiphyton excretion rate [EE=0.000] < 0.001 for epiphyton group 1
Oxygen to algal respiration stoichiometry [O2AR=1.400] /= 1.1 for waterbody 1
Phosphorus/organic matter stoichiometry [ORGP=0.011] /= 0.005 for waterbody 1
Water surface elevation is below bottom elevation at segment 1
Water surface elevation is below bottom elevation at segment 2

    Warning Messages

    The absence of warning messages does not guarantee that all inputs are correct. Not all possible input combinations can be checked. Do not assume that because there are no warnings generated there are no problems with inputs.

    Error Messages (pre.err)¶

    Input errors that will prevent the model from running correctly or running at all are extensively screened and, when found, are included in the preprocessor error output file. All error messages include a concise statement in English along with the Fortran variable name and its input value.

    Example:

    Starting time [TMSTRT=75.000] < ending time [TMEND=-320.000]
Timestep fraction [DLTF(1)=-0.850] <= 0.0
Internal gravity wave limitation control  [CELC= on] /= " ON" or "OFF" for waterbody 1
Heat exchange solution control [SLHTC= term] /= " TERM" or " ET" for waterbody 1
Vertical advection time weighting [THETA=-0.550] < 0.0 for waterbody 1
Selective withdrawal elevation [ESTR=137.700] < the bottom active cell elevation

    Spreadsheet Profile Plot¶

    The spreadsheet profile output was designed for import into a spreadsheet program for plotting vertical profiles of temperature, constituents, and derived constituents. Output consists of a comma-delimited file with the variable name, Julian date, depth below water surface, elevation, and temperature, horizontal velocity, horizontal layer flow, and/or concentrations for the output segment. Additional segments each contain a depth, elevation, and temperature/concentration column and are continued to the right. Whenever a depth or vertical layer is part of the channel bottom, a value of -99 is placed in the cell.

    Constituent,Julian_day,Depth,Elevation,Seg_9,Elevation,Seg_10,Elevation,Seg_11,Elevation,
Temperature(C),  1.500,  1.549, 439.649,  6.611, 439.649,  6.658, 439.649,  6.664,
Temperature(C),  1.500,  4.073, 437.125,  6.611, 437.125,  6.658, 437.125,  6.664,
HorizontalVelocity(ms-1), 1.500, 1.549, 439.649, -0.0001, 439.649, -0.0036, 439.649, 0.0007,
HorizontalLayerFlow(m3s-1), 1.500, 1.549, 439.649, -0.0936, 439.649, -4.1658, 439.649, 0.5209,

    If SPRC is set to ONV rather than ON, an additional output file is written that contains the volume-weighted temperature, water quality state, and derived variables at the locations and times specified. This file has the suffix _volw.csv and is comma delimited.

    Constituent Julian day Seg_35 Seg_36
    Temperature 1.04 4 8.986
    Age 1.04 0 0
    Temperature 31.041 3.998 3.999
    Age 31.041 14.335 16.312

    Profile Plot¶

    The profile plot output file (prf.opt) was originally developed to provide data for a plotting package called DISSPLA from Computer Associates. The longitudinal profile plot output format is described in the next section.

    As in most other output files, the card titles are included for identifying the run. The first line after the titles reports:

    1. Maximum number of layers (KMX)
    2. Number of segments for which information is output (NIPRF)
    3. Total number of output dates (NDSP)
    4. Total number of constituents (NCT)
    5. Total number of derived constituents (NDC)
    6. Pointer to the profile date currently in effect (PRFDP)
    7. Waterbody surface layer (KTWB)
    8. Logical variable: T if constituents are simulated, otherwise F
      39      1      3     17     23     12      1     23 T

    The next line reports the segment(s) to output [IPRF]. The next three lines report which constituents are active. Constituent and derived constituent names follow in the next 21 lines. The constituent and derived constituent numbers follow on the next line. Temperature is always considered constituent 1. The bottom active layer for each output segment is given on the next line.

    Layer heights for layers 1 through KMX are output next. Initial temperatures/constituent concentrations for each segment specified in the profile segment card are then output. The first line contains the constituent abbreviation and the number of active layers. The next line(s) contain the values for a given segment.

    TEMP      14
    5.50      5.50      5.50      5.40      5.40      5.50      5.40      5.30
    5.30      5.40      5.30      5.20      5.20      5.20

    The next output grouping consists of temperature/constituent concentrations over time. The first line includes Julian date (JDAY), Gregorian date (GDAY), water surface layer number (KTWB), deviation of the water surface (Z) from the top of the surface layer (KT), and how many dates information has been written.

      75.702 Mar 15, 1992    23 -0.1950     1
TEMP      14
    5.81      5.64      5.48      5.41      5.37      5.35      5.34      5.33
    5.32      5.32      5.32      5.32      5.32      5.32
ISS       14
 0.674E+00 0.850E+00 0.991E+00 0.116E+01 0.136E+01 0.148E+01 0.166E+01 0.191E+01
 0.208E+01 0.225E+01 0.239E+01 0.250E+01 0.255E+01 0.255E+01

    Longitudinal Profile Plot¶

    An alternative output for the profile plot is the longitudinal profile plot. This is set in the profile plot section of the control file. The output consists of files named ProfLongJDXXX.csv where XXX is the Julian day of the profile. The output consists of model segment number, water surface elevation (m), flow rate (m³ s⁻¹), temperature (°C) of the surface layer, depth (m), width (m), volume-weighted temperature of the segment (°C), and concentrations of state variables and derived variables at the surface layer. The file is comma delimited.

    Seg#,ElevWaterSurf(m),Q(m3/s),SurfaceTemp(oC),Depth(m),Width(m),VolWeighTemp(oC),
    TDS,     Gen1,    Gen2,    Gen3,    ISS1,    PO4,     NH4,     NO3,     Fe,
    LDOM,    RDOM,    LPOM,    RPOM,    ALG1,    DO,      TIC,     ALK,
  2,   122.335,    3.782,   28.740,    3.985,   79.000,   28.529,   25.161,
100.000,    0.762,   51.823,   41.164,    6.043,  108.891,    0.014,    0.107,
  4.776,   14.229,    0.330,    0.002,    4.293,   14.829,   46.230,

    Vector Plot¶

    The vector plot output writes a binary file that is used by the W2_POST post-processor developed by DSI, Inc. Use the filetype w2L for W2 Linkage file so that W2_Post.exe can recognize the file natively. Only the first filename for the first waterbody is used for the output for all waterbodies. The binary file contains velocity field data (horizontal and vertical velocities) at each computational cell for each output time, used to create velocity vector overlays on contour plots.

    Contour Plot¶

    The contour plot output (cpl.opt) was originally included for developing DISSPLA contour plots of model output using Fortran callable subroutines. Output consists initially of time-invariant grid information:

    1. Title cards
    2. Number of branches (NBR)
    3. Grid dimensions (KMX and IMX)
    4. Branch upstream and downstream segments (US and DS)
    5. Segment length (DLX)
    6. Layer heights (H)
    7. Number of active constituents
    8. Active constituent names

    During simulations, the following time-varying information is output:

    1. Julian date (JDAY) and Gregorian date (GDAY)
    2. Water surface layer (KTWB)
    3. Tributary inflows (QTR) and temperatures (TTR)
    4. Tributary inflow concentrations for each active constituent (CTR)
    5. Current upstream segment (CUS)
    6. Branch inflow (QIN) and the sum of the branch inflow (QSUM)
    7. Interfacial area (BHRKT1) for all active segments in the surface layer (KT)
    8. Interfacial area (BHR) for all remaining active computational cells
    9. Horizontal velocities (U)
    10. Flows (QC)
    11. Water surface deviations (Z)
    12. Temperatures
    13. Constituent concentrations

    The user must develop their own plotting program to use this feature unless the TECPLOT option is chosen.

    TECPLOT Output¶

    When the TECPLOT option is ON, the output consists of elevation (m), longitudinal distance (m), U (x-velocity, m s⁻¹), W (z-velocity, m s⁻¹), T (°C), RHO (kg m⁻³), Fish Habitat number, and concentrations for all active constituents for the entire grid at the time interval specified in CPL FREQ. Tecplot can read the file directly and the user can create contour animations of any variables including superimposed velocity vector plots.

    The Fish Habitat number is the fish criterion satisfied at each grid point (K,I). Define fish criteria in order of more restrictive to least restrictive:

    OptimalFishX,12.0,18.0,5.0
SuboptimalFishX,10.0,22.0,5.0
NonOptimalFishX(Lethal),0.0,35.0,4.0

    The first fish is given #1, second #2, and 3rd fish group #3. For each grid point, the habitat criteria value 1, 2, 3, or 100 (if outside the last range) is written to the Tecplot output file.

    To create a contour animation in Tecplot:

    1. Import data (cpl.opt) into Tecplot using the Tecplot Data Loader.
    2. Turn ON contours.
    3. Reset axes so that they are Independent (Tecplot default is dependent).
    4. Turn on Value Blanking since state variables outside the domain are set to -99.
    5. Reset the contour intervals since Tecplot automatically uses -99 as its minimum data value.
    6. Go to the animation and view the zones on screen or export to an AVI file.
    7. To add vectors, turn on vectors and choose U and W as the vector variables.

    Kinetic Fluxes¶

    The kinetic flux output provides detailed information about kinetic flux rates at specified segments. Two output types are available:

    • Formatted text output (similar to snapshot file format): Fluxes at segments specified by SNP SEG.
    • CSV output (kflux_wb#.csv): Summed fluxes for all active cells at the frequency specified by FLX FREQ.

    There are 142 available kinetic flux terms covering all major kinetic processes:

    # Short Name Description
    1 TISSIN ISS settling in, kg day⁻¹
    2 TISSOUT ISS settling out, kg day⁻¹
    3 PO4AR PO₄ algal respiration, kg day⁻¹
    4 PO4AG PO₄ algal growth, kg day⁻¹
    5 PO4AP PO₄ algal net, kg day⁻¹
    6 PO4ER PO₄ epiphyton respiration, kg day⁻¹
    7 PO4EG PO₄ epiphyton growth, kg day⁻¹
    8 PO4EP PO₄ epiphyton net, kg day⁻¹
    9 PO4POM PO₄ POM decay, kg day⁻¹
    10 PO4DOM PO₄ DOM decay, kg day⁻¹
    11 PO4OM PO₄ OM decay, kg day⁻¹
    12 PO4SED PO₄ sediment decay, kg day⁻¹
    13 PO4SOD PO₄ SOD release, kg day⁻¹
    14 PO4SET PO₄ net settling, kg day⁻¹
    15 NH4NITR NH₄ nitrification, kg day⁻¹
    16 NH4AR NH₄ algal respiration, kg day⁻¹
    17 NH4AG NH₄ algal growth, kg day⁻¹
    18 NH4AP NH₄ algal net, kg day⁻¹
    19 NH4ER NH₄ epiphyton respiration, kg day⁻¹
