    Flux Numbering

    Fluxes 1--72 can be toggled ON/OFF by the model user on the CST FLUX card. Fluxes 73--120 are automatically computed when the relevant processes are active. Fluxes 121--142 are automatically turned on when sediment diagenesis, H₂S, CH₄, Fe, or Mn processes are active.

    Withdrawal Outflow¶

    Whenever withdrawal output control [WDOC] is ON (see w2_con.npt or w2_con.csv file), withdrawal outflow files contain information for plotting any release or withdrawal temperatures and/or constituent concentrations as a time series. The files can also be used to externally link upstream waterbodies to downstream waterbodies so that waterbasins can be broken up into multiple waterbodies. This is an important feature when runtimes for the entire waterbasin become excessive. With this option, calibration can start at the most upstream waterbody and proceed sequentially downstream.

    The model user can specify the output filenames by specifying WDOFN (see Withdrawal Output (WITH OUT) card). WDOFN can describe a subdirectory and filename. For example, if WDOFN=.\wdo files\Lake1.csv, then the output files will be written to a subdirectory called wdo files.

    Integrated Output¶

    Output consists of up to four separate files for each segment [IWDO] specified in the control file:

    File Type Contents
    q_AAA_segXX.YYY Flow rates (m³ s⁻¹)
    t_AAA_segXX.YYY Temperatures (°C)
    c_AAA_segXX.YYY Constituent concentrations
    d_AAA_segXX.YYY Derived constituent concentrations

    Where AAA is the withdrawal output filename entered for WDOFN, XX is the segment number (IWDO), and YYY is the filetype given for the WDO output file. These files are for all the withdrawals specified at that segment lumped together if there are multiple withdrawals. The constituent concentration outflow file is only generated if constituent computations [CCC] are turned on. The derived constituent concentration outflow file is generated only if constituent computations are turned on and one or more derived constituents [CDWBC] are turned on.

    For the flow and temperature files, individual flows and outlet temperatures are provided to the right of the weighted average (temperature) or summed flow. All output files are in comma-delimited format and are in the correct format for a downstream inflow or tributary file.

    Individual Output¶

    A series of individual comma-delimited files are also output for each structure at the withdrawal segment. For example, if there are 2 spillways (weirs), 1 gate and 1 pump at the withdrawal segment, all these withdrawal types are automatically output individually as separate files with a fixed internally specified file name based on the hydraulic element and the segment number, such as: qwo_sp1_seg23.csv, qwo_sp2_seg23.csv, qwo_gate1_seg23.csv, and qwo_pmp1_seg23.csv. These files have only flow, temperature, and concentration for one outlet. Similarly named output files are provided for gates, spillways, and pipes if they are located at the withdrawal output segment [IWDO].

    WDO Output

    WDO output files are not multiplied by CMULT or CDMULT, unlike the snapshot and time series output files.

    Mass Balance Output File¶

    When Mass_Balance [MBC] is turned ON (see CALCULATIONS in control file), the model outputs a summary of the N and P mass balance for each waterbody based on the output interval for CPL output with the name massbal.csv. If CPL output is turned OFF, [CPLC]=OFF, or if [MBC] is OFF or if no derived variables were turned ON (TP, total phosphorus, and TN, total nitrogen, must be turned ON), then no file will be written. This file is useful in looking at waterbody overall N and P balances. The file includes:

    • Julian day and waterbody number
    • Total phosphorus and total nitrogen in water column (kg), sediments (kg), and plants (kg)
    • Cumulative TP and TN in outflow, tributaries, distributed tributaries, withdrawals, precipitation, and inflow (kg)
    • Cumulative sediment release and flux to sediments (kg)
    • Sediment diagenesis flux terms (kg)

    Flow Balance Output File¶

    When Volume_Balance [VBC] is ON (see CALCULATIONS in control file), the model outputs a summary of the flow balance for each waterbody based on the output interval for Contour output or CPL output in a file named flowbalance.csv. If CPL output is turned OFF, [CPLC]=OFF, or Volume_Balance [VBC] is OFF, then no file will be written. This output file is useful in looking at waterbody overall flows. The output file consists of the following information:

    • Julian day and waterbody number
    • Inflow volume to waterbody from Qin files in m³
    • Inflow volume from precipitation in m³
    • Outflow volume from outlet structures in m³
    • Output volume from withdrawals in m³
    • Output volume from evaporation in m³
    • Input volume from distributed tributaries in m³
    • Input volume from tributaries in m³
    • Volume of ice formation/ice melting in m³ (this is zero if [ICEC] is OFF)
    • Percent error in computing the fluid mass balance

    The volumes and percent error between Julian days are cumulative, not instantaneous, and are negative for an outflow and positive for an inflow. Note that if one has ice formation removing water and later ice melting back into the waterbody, there should not be any net gain or loss of water.

    SYSTDG Output File¶

    TDG (Total Dissolved Gas) concentrations computed with the SYSTDG equations are written into the withdrawal output files. Withdrawal outflow files contain information with release or withdrawal TDG concentrations in the time series output files. The model output consists of up to four separate files for the individual segment [IWDO] specified in the W2 control file. The files include an outflow, outflow temperature, constituent concentrations, and derived constituent concentrations. These files are for all the withdrawals specified at that segment lumped together if there are multiple withdrawals. All output files are in comma-delimited format. Also, whenever withdrawal output is ON, a series of individual files is also written in the output file for each structure at the withdrawal segment.

    An additional TDG target output file, TDGTarget_output.opt, is generated from the model if TDGTA is turned ON. This file contains information for outflow release or spillway TDG concentrations and outflows as a time series file.

    Lake River Contour Plots Output Files¶

    When the lake_river_contour.csv input file is present, the model produces output files for lake and river contour plots:

    • LakeContour_DO_SegXX.csv: Lake contour for dissolved oxygen at segment XX
    • Lake_Contour_T_SegXX.csv: Lake contour for temperature at segment XX
    • RiverContour_DO_BrYY.csv: River contour for dissolved oxygen for branch YY
    • RiverContour_T_BrYY.csv: River contour for temperature for branch YY

    Two output formats are available:

    1. Format 1: Columns of JDAY, elevation (or distance), and temperature/dissolved oxygen (DO) value
    2. Format 2: Elevations (or distances) in the first row; rows are time steps with temperature/DO values at each elevation (or distance)

    Water Level Output File¶

    A convenient file for plotting water level variations as a function of time and space is written out if the TIME_SERIES is ON. The output file name is wl.csv. The file consists of Julian day followed by the water surface elevation at each model segment in m. The water level at every model segment is written rather than those specified in the TIME_SERIES output. An example file is shown below:

    JDAY,SEG    2,SEG    3,SEG    4,...,SEG   65,SEG
1.001,1484.700,1484.700,1484.700,...
1.150,1484.700,1484.700,...

    W2Anim Output File¶

    If the user uses Stewart Rounds' W2Anim post-processor (https://github.com/sarounds/w2anim/releases/latest), one of the files used for viewing the flow, temperature, and water quality of outlets is now automatically output. This file is used for viewing the outlet dynamics (flow, temperature, and water quality) for any outflow. The output file is written whenever there is a WDO (withdrawal output) specified.

    The file is internally named based on the prefix and suffix specified for the WDO output file. The file name is qwo_layers_XX_wdo.csv if the prefix was wdo and the suffix was csv, where XX is the segment number where the withdrawal occurs. The file contains:

    • Row 1: Header identifying the file as "Flow layers file for segment XX"
    • Row 2: Column headers including "Output is total outflow, WS elev, El(Kbm)" and "layer outflows starting w/ layer 2"
    • Row 3: Column labels for JDAY, Q(m3/s), El(Kbm), and LayerFlow(m3/s)
    • Subsequent rows: Time series of Julian day, total outflow, water surface elevation, bottom elevation, and flow in each layer starting with layer 2

    The column D starts with the flow in layer 2, column E is the flow in layer 3, and so on.

    Run-time Warnings¶

    The model generates the following run-time warning messages that can be useful when setting up the model and debugging errors that may have occurred. These are output to the file w2.wrn.

    1. Water surface is above the top of layer 2 in segment 'x' at day 'y'. This message informs the user that the water surface elevation has gone above the top of the computational grid. This may be okay or it might indicate that additional layers need to be added to the grid so that the surface layer does not become inappropriately thick. This is a common message when first calibrating the water surface in a reservoir if either the inflows are overestimated or the outflows are underestimated.

    2. Computational warning at Julian day = 'x,' at segment 'y' timestep = 'z' water surface deviation [Z] = 'x' m layer thickness = 'y' m. This message indicates that the water surface elevation solution is starting to go unstable as a result of velocities approaching supercritical values. The water surface elevation has gone from residing in layer KT to layer KT+1, which causes a negative surface layer thickness. The surface layer thickness, HKT1, is the layer thickness, H, minus the water surface deviation, z, from the top layer KT. This is negative when the solution begins to go unstable.

      Normally, when the water surface elevation approaches the bottom of layer KT, the model would subtract a layer thus allowing the water surface level to decline smoothly over time. However, if the water surface elevation jumps below the bottom of layer KT in one timestep, the layer subtraction algorithm does not have a chance to adjust the water surface layer before solving for the velocities.

    3. Unstable water surface elevation on day 'x,' negative surface layer thickness using minimum timestep at iteration 'y'. The model attempts to recover from the instability by decreasing the timestep by 90% and recomputing the water surface elevation. If the current timestep is greater than the minimum timestep [DLTMIN], then this warning message is generated. If the current timestep is less than or equal to [DLTMIN], then an error message is output and the run is stopped. To recover, reduce [DLTMAX] and/or [DLTF] just prior to the instability and then increase back to original values later in the simulation.

    4. Low water in segment 'x' water surface deviation ='y' at day 'z'. This warning message alerts the user that water is drying up in a segment with only one active layer. This may mean that more friction is required to hold back water in downstream segments or the segment in question, or that segments upstream may need to have less friction.

    5. Computational warning at Julian day = 'x' spatial change ='x' m³ temporal change ='y' m³ volume error ='z' m³. This message lets the user know that the volume error is more than 1000 m³. This should not normally happen unless the user has incorrectly modified the code or the water surface elevation solution has become unstable.

    6. Change of model surface layer by adding or subtracting layers. Informational diagnostic message.

    7. Other diagnostic information.

    Instability Recovery

    If the instability is occurring during high inflow periods, the user can spread inflows out over a greater time period to reduce maximum velocities. Care must be taken to ensure that the total inflows remain the same, especially if inflow interpolation [QINIC] is turned on. Velocities can also be reduced through adjustments to the bathymetry such as making cells wider. If inflows are placed according to density [PQINC] and/or [PQTRC], velocities can also be reduced by using the "distribute evenly from top to bottom" option. Another option is to increase the minimum number of layers [NLMIN] for a segment to remain active.

    Run-time Errors¶

    When the model goes unstable and stops running, the code generates two files: w2.err and w2errordump.csv. The model generates the run-time error messages and outputs them to w2.err, and generates an error "dump" file of all the pertinent output at the time of the model crash, w2errordump.csv. Both of these files are useful in debugging the cause of a model instability and "crash."

    W2.err¶

    Error 1: Unstable water surface elevation on day 'x' negative surface layer thickness using minimum timestep at iteration 'y'

    As discussed in the Run-time Warnings section, this error message is generated by an unstable water surface elevation solution. Additional information output to the error file includes the segment number, the water surface elevation at that segment, the bottom elevation of that segment, and the elevation difference for all segments in the offending branch.

    The model user may need to lower DLTMAX forcing the model to run at a lower time step around the period of instability. Or perhaps the model is drying up, and there is insufficient water in the main branch. This involves reassessing the inflows and outflows to your waterbody. Examine all preprocessor warnings in case there were no errors and check each one to see if that is a cause of the problem.

    Error 2: Fatal error: Insufficient segments in branch 'x' Julian day = 'y' water surface layer = 'z'

    Currently in the model, if the number of segments in the main branch becomes less than two due to drying out and subsequent subtraction of upstream segments from the active computational grid, then the model issues this error message and terminates. There must be at least two active segments in the main branch for many of the computations to proceed. This error is most common in reservoir simulations. For all side-branches off the mainstem branch, a branch can go inactive if it dries up.

    If this occurs during water surface calibration due to insufficient inflows or excessive outflows, then adjust the flows so that either more water comes in or less water flows out. Another workaround is to add additional depth to the bottom of the branch so that water levels can drop without segments being subtracted. Similarly, the number of active layers required to prevent segment subtraction [NLMIN] can be set to one if it is not already set to that value.

    Example:

    Unstable water surface elevation on day 200.039
negative surface layer thickness using minimum timestep at iteration 295
Branch #:          1  in Waterbody:          1  Surface layer KT:         39
Segment, Surface layer thickness, m, Flow m3/s, U(KT,I) m/s, ELWS, m
    37             3.15           92.69       0.63      495.73
    38             3.09           95.95       0.76      494.66
    39             3.00       -12781.80      -0.30      493.54
    40           -88.37      3541556.07  -19427.77      401.16
    41             9.73            0.00       0.02      498.24

    W2ErrorDump.csv¶

    This file provides comprehensive diagnostic information at the time of model crash. The variable names shown in the dump file are as follows:

    Variable Description
    JDAY Julian day at time of model crash
    QIN Inflow flow rates in m³ s⁻¹ for each model branch
    QTR Tributary inflow flow rates in m³ s⁻¹ for each tributary
    QDT Distributed inflow flow rates in m³ s⁻¹ for each branch
    QWD Withdrawal flow rates in m³ s⁻¹ for each withdrawal
    SEG Segment number (I)
    BRANCH Branch number
    KT Surface layer
    WSE Water surface elevation in m
    SZ Prior time step deviation of the surface layer water surface from KT
    Z Current time step deviation of the surface layer water surface from KT
    Q Flow rate between model segments computed from horizontal momentum equation
    QC Corrected flow rate between model segments computed from continuity balance (this is what is used in the model)
    QERR Differences in flow rate between Q and QC
    H2KT Prior time step surface layer depth
    H1KT Current time step surface layer depth
    BHR1 Current time step right hand side area (depth times width) of the surface layer
    BHR2 Prior time step right hand side area (depth times width) of the surface layer
    T1 Current time step model temperature at the surface layer
    T2 Prior time step model temperature at the surface layer
    SUKT Prior time step velocity of the surface layer at the right-hand side of the segment
    UKT Current time step velocity of the surface layer at the right-hand side of the segment
